Ref.[52]for details):U^​f=∑k=0∞(−λ)k​(x3)2​k[[k]]q−4!​q−2​n^3​(n^++n^−+k)​Dq−4,x+k​Dq−4,x−k​f.\hat{U}f=\sum_{k\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{\infty}\left(-\lambda\right)^{k}\frac{(x^{3})^{2k}}{[[k]]_{q^{-4}}!}\,q^{-2\hat{n}_{3}(\hat{n}_{+}+\hskip 0.72229pt\hat{n}_{-}+\hskip 0.72229ptk)}D_{q^{-4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}}^{k}D_{q^{-4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{-}}^{k}f.(255)

The inverse operatorU−1U^{-1}is given by:U^−1​f=∑k=0∞λk​(x3)2​k[[k]]q4!​q2​n^3​(n^++n^−+k)​Dq4,x+k​Dq4,x−k​f.\hat{U}^{-1}f=\sum_{k\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{\infty}\lambda^{k}\hskip 0.72229pt\frac{(x^{3})^{2k}}{[[k]]_{q^{4}}!}\,q^{2\hat{n}_{3}(\hat{n}_{+}+\hskip 0.72229pt\hat{n}_{-}+\hskip 0.72229ptk)}D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}}^{k}D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{-}}^{k}f.(256)

## Appendix BDerivatives and Integrals onqq-Deformed Euclidean
Space

Partial derivatives can be consistently defined forqq-deformed space-time
coordinates[53,54]. Theseqq-deformed partial derivativesobey the same commutation relations
as the covariant coordinate generatorsXiX_{i}:∂0∂+=∂+∂0,∂0∂−=∂−∂0,∂0∂3=∂3∂0,\displaystyle\partial_{0}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{+}=\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{+}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{0},\quad\partial_{0}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{-}=\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{-}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{0},\quad\partial_{0}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3}=\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{0},∂+∂3=q2​∂3∂+,∂3∂−=q2​∂−∂3,\displaystyle\partial_{+}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3}=q^{2}\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{+},\quad\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{-}=\hskip 0.72229ptq^{2}\partial_{-}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3},∂+∂−−∂−∂+=λ​∂3∂3.\displaystyle\partial_{+}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{-}-\partial_{-}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{+}=\hskip 0.72229pt\lambda\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3}.(257)

There are two consistent ways to commuteqq-deformed partial derivatives
withqq-deformed coordinates. The correspondingqq-deformed
Leibniz rulestake the form[53,54,46]:∂BXA=δBA+q4​R^​XDA​CB​D​∂C,\displaystyle\partial_{B}X^{A}=\delta_{B}^{A}+q^{4}\hat{R}{{}^{AC}}_{BD}\,X^{D}\partial_{C},∂AX0=X0​∂A,∂0XA=XA​∂0,\displaystyle\partial_{A}X^{0}=X^{0}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{A},\quad\partial_{0}\hskip 0.72229ptX^{A}=X^{A}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{0},∂0X0=1+X0​∂0.\displaystyle\partial_{0}\hskip 0.72229ptX^{0}=1+X^{0}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{0}.(258)

HereR^B​DA​C\hat{R}{{}^{AC}}_{BD}denotes the vector representation of the R-matrix
associated with three-dimensionalqq-deformed Euclidean space[18]. By redefining∂^A=q6​∂A\hat{\partial}_{A}=q^{6}\partial_{A}and∂^0=∂0\hat{\partial}_{0}=\partial_{0}, theLeibniz rules of the second
differential calculus can be written as∂^B​XA=δBA+q−4​(R^−1)​XDA​CB​D​∂^C,\displaystyle\hat{\partial}_{B}\hskip 0.72229ptX^{A}=\delta_{B}^{A}+q^{-4}(\hat{R}^{-1}){{}^{AC}}_{BD}\,X^{D}\hat{\partial}_{C},∂^A​X0=X0​∂^A,∂^0​XA=XA​∂^0,\displaystyle\hat{\partial}_{A}\hskip 0.72229ptX^{0}=X^{0}\hskip 0.72229pt\hat{\partial}_{A},\quad\hat{\partial}_{0}\hskip 0.72229ptX^{A}=X^{A}\hskip 0.72229pt\hat{\partial}_{0},∂^0​X0=1+X0​∂^0.\displaystyle\hat{\partial}_{0}\hskip 0.72229ptX^{0}=1+X^{0}\hskip 0.72229pt\hat{\partial}_{0}.(259)

Using either set ofqq-deformed Leibniz rules, one can compute the action
ofqq-deformed partial derivatives on normal-ordered monomials of
noncommutative coordinates. This action is then extended to commutative
coordinates via the Moyal-Weyl map𝒲\mathcal{W}:∂i⊳(x+)n+​(x3)n3​(x−)n−​tn0=𝒲−1​(∂i⊳(X+)n+​(X3)n3​(X−)n−​(X0)n0).\partial_{i}\triangleright(x^{+})^{n_{+}}(x^{3})^{n_{3}}(x^{-})^{n_{-}}t^{\hskip 0.72229ptn_{0}}=\mathcal{W}^{\hskip 0.72229pt-1}\big(\partial_{i}\triangleright(X^{+})^{n_{+}}(X^{3})^{n_{3}}(X^{-})^{n_{-}}(X^{0})^{n_{0}}\big).(260)

Since the Moyal-Weyl map is linear, this prescription naturally extends to
power series in commutative space-time coordinates:∂i⊳f​(𝐱,t)=𝒲−1​(∂i⊳𝒲​(f​(𝐱,t))).\partial_{i}\triangleright f(\mathbf{x},t)=\mathcal{W}^{\hskip 0.72229pt-1}\big(\partial_{i}\triangleright\mathcal{W}(f(\mathbf{x},t))\big).(261)

For normal-ordered monomials as defined in Eq. (244), the
Leibniz rules in Eq. (258) yield explicit operator
representations[55]:∂+⊳f​(𝐱,t)\displaystyle\partial_{+}\triangleright f(\mathbf{x},t)=Dq4,x+​f​(𝐱,t),\displaystyle=D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}}f(\mathbf{x},t),∂3⊳f​(𝐱,t)\displaystyle\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3}\triangleright f(\mathbf{x},t)=Dq2,x3​f​(q2​x+,x3,x−,t),\displaystyle=D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}f(q^{2}x^{+},x^{3},x^{-},t),∂−⊳f​(𝐱,t)\displaystyle\partial_{-}\triangleright f(\mathbf{x},t)=Dq4,x−​f​(x+,q2​x3,x−,t)+λ​x+​Dq2,x32​f​(𝐱,t).\displaystyle=D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{-}}f(x^{+},q^{2}x^{3},x^{-},t)+\lambda\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{2}f(\mathbf{x},t).(262)

The derivative∂0\partial_{0}is represented on the commutative space-time
algebra by the standard time derivative:∂0⊳f​(𝐱,t)=∂f​(𝐱,t)∂t.\partial_{0}\triangleright\hskip-0.72229ptf(\mathbf{x},t)=\frac{\partial f(\mathbf{x},t)}{\partial t}.(263)

Certain calculations require evaluating the action of higher powers ofqq-deformed partial derivatives on a power series in commutative space-time
coordinates. Using the operator representations given in
Eq. (262), we obtain (form+,m3∈ℕ0m_{+},m_{3}\in\mathbb{N}_{0})(∂+)m+⊳f\displaystyle(\partial_{+})^{m_{+}}\triangleright f=Dq4,x+m+​f,\displaystyle=D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}}^{m_{+}}f,(∂3)m3⊳f\displaystyle(\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3})^{m_{3}}\triangleright f=Dq2,x3m3​f​(q2​m3​x+),\displaystyle=D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{m_{3}}f(q^{2m_{3}}x^{+}),(264)

and (form−∈ℕ0m_{-}\in\mathbb{N}_{0})(∂−)m−⊳f\displaystyle(\partial_{-})^{m_{-}}\triangleright f=[q2​n^3​Dq4,x−+λ​x+​Dq2,x32]m−​f\displaystyle=\left[\hskip 0.72229ptq^{2\hat{n}_{3}}D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{-}}+\hskip 0.72229pt\lambda\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{2}\right]^{m_{-}}f=∑i=0m−λi​[m−i]q−4​(x+)i​Dq2,x32​i​(Dq4,x−​q2​n^3)m−−i​f\displaystyle=\sum_{i\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{m_{-}}\hskip 0.72229pt\lambda^{i}\genfrac{\left[}{]}{0.0pt}{}{m_{-}}{i}_{q^{-4}}(x^{+})^{i}D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{2i}\big(D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{-}}q^{2\hat{n}_{3}}\big)^{m_{-}-\hskip 0.72229pti}f=∑i=0m−λi​[m−i]q−4​(x+)i​Dq2,x32​i​Dq4,x−m−−i​f​(q2​(m−−i)​x3).\displaystyle=\sum_{i\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{m_{-}}\hskip 0.72229pt\lambda^{i}\genfrac{\left[}{]}{0.0pt}{}{m_{-}}{i}_{q^{-4}}(x^{+})^{i}D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{2i}D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{-}}^{m_{-}-\hskip 0.72229pti}f(q^{2(m_{-}-\hskip 0.72229pti)}x^{3}).(265)

The second identity in the calculation above follows from the commutation
relation(Dq4,x−​q2​n^3)​(λ​x+​Dq2,x32)=q−4​(λ​x+​Dq2,x32)​(Dq4,x−​q2​n^3),\big(D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{-}}q^{2\hat{n}_{3}}\big)\big(\lambda\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{2}\big)=q^{-4}\big(\lambda\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{2}\big)\big(D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{-}}q^{2\hat{n}_{3}}\big),(266)

together with theqq-binomial theorem[29]:(x+a)n=∑k=0n[nk]q​xk​an−kifa​x=q​x​a.(x+a)^{n}=\sum_{k\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{n}\genfrac{\left[}{]}{0.0pt}{}{n}{k}_{q}x^{k}a^{n-k}\quad\text{if}\quad a\hskip 0.72229ptx=q\hskip 0.72229ptxa.(267)

Combining both results in Eq. (264), one finds(∂3)m3⁡(∂+)m+⊳f\displaystyle(\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3})^{m_{3}}(\partial_{+})^{m_{+}}\triangleright f=Dq2,x3m3​q2​m3​n^+​Dq4,x+m+​f\displaystyle=D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{m_{3}}\hskip 0.72229ptq^{2m_{3}\hat{n}_{+}}D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}}^{m_{+}}f=q−2​m3​m+​Dq2,x3m3​Dq4,x+m+​q2​m3​n^+​f\displaystyle=q^{-2m_{3}m_{+}}D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{m_{3}}\hskip 0.72229ptD_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}}^{m_{+}}\hskip 0.72229ptq^{2m_{3}\hat{n}_{+}}f=q−2​m3​m+​Dq2,x3m3​Dq4,x+m+​f​(q2​m3​x+).\displaystyle=q^{-2m_{3}m_{+}}D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{m_{3}}\hskip 0.72229ptD_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}}^{m_{+}}f(q^{2m_{3}}x^{+}).(268)

Using the results from Eqs. (265) and (268),
the combined action is given by(∂−)m−⁡(∂3)m3⁡(∂+)m+⊳f=∑i=0m−λi​[m−i]q−4​q−2​m3​(m++m−−i)​(x+)i\displaystyle(\partial_{-})^{m_{-}}(\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3})^{m_{3}}(\partial_{+})^{m_{+}}\triangleright f=\sum_{i\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{m_{-}}\hskip 0.72229pt\lambda^{i}\genfrac{\left[}{]}{0.0pt}{}{m_{-}}{i}_{q^{-4}}q^{-2m_{3}(m_{+}+\hskip 0.72229ptm_{-}-\hskip 0.72229pti)}(x^{+})^{i}×Dq4,x−m−−i​Dq2,x3m3+2​i​Dq4,x+m+​f​(q2​m3​x+,q2​(m−−i)​x3)\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{-}}^{m_{-}-\hskip 0.72229pti}D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{m_{3}+2i}D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}}^{m_{+}}f(q^{2m_{3}}x^{+},q^{2(m_{-}-\hskip 0.72229pti)}x^{3})=q−2​m3​(m++m−)​Dq4,x−m−​Dq2,x3m3​Dq4,x+m+​f​(q2​m3​x+,q2​m−​x3)+𝒪​(h).\displaystyle=q^{-2m_{3}(m_{+}+\hskip 0.72229ptm_{-})}D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{-}}^{m_{-}}D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{m_{3}}\hskip 0.72229ptD_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}}^{m_{+}}f(q^{2m_{3}}x^{+},q^{2m_{-}}x^{3})+\mathcal{O}(h).(269)

The operator representations for the partial derivatives∂^i\hat{\partial}_{i}can be derived from the Leibniz rules in Eq. (259).
The Leibniz rules in Eqs. (258) and
(259) are related by the substitutionsq→q−1,X−→X+,X+→X−,\displaystyle q\rightarrow q^{-1},\quad X^{-}\rightarrow X^{+},\quad X^{+}\rightarrow X^{-},∂+→∂^−,∂−→∂^+,∂3→∂^3,∂0→∂^0.\displaystyle\partial_{+}\rightarrow\hat{\partial}_{-},\quad\partial_{-}\rightarrow\hat{\partial}_{+},\quad\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3}\rightarrow\hat{\partial}_{\hskip 0.72229pt3},\quad\partial_{0}\rightarrow\hat{\partial}_{0}.(270)

Thus, the operator representations of the partial derivatives∂^A\hat{\partial}_{A}can be obtained from those of∂A\partial_{A}[cf.
Eq. (262)] by replacingqqwithq−1q^{-1}and interchanging the
indices++and−-:∂^−​⊳¯​f​(𝐱,t)\displaystyle\hat{\partial}_{-}\,\bar{\triangleright}\,f(\mathbf{x},t)=Dq−4,x−​f​(𝐱,t),\displaystyle=D_{q^{-4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{-}}f(\mathbf{x},t),∂^3​⊳¯​f​(𝐱,t)\displaystyle\hat{\partial}_{\hskip 0.72229pt3}\,\bar{\triangleright}\,f(\mathbf{x},t)=Dq−2,x3​f​(q−2​x−,x3,x+,t),\displaystyle=D_{q^{-2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}f(q^{-2}x^{-},x^{3},x^{+},t),∂^+​⊳¯​f​(𝐱,t)\displaystyle\hat{\partial}_{+}\,\bar{\triangleright}\,f(\mathbf{x},t)=Dq−4,x+​f​(x−,q−2​x3,x+,t)−λ​x−​Dq−2,x32​f​(𝐱,t).\displaystyle=D_{q^{-4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}}f(x^{-},q^{-2}x^{3},x^{+},t)-\lambda\hskip 0.72229ptx^{-}D_{q^{-2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{2}f(\mathbf{x},t).(271)

Finally, the time derivative∂^0\hat{\partial}_{0}acts on the commutative
space-time algebra as the usual derivative with respect tott:∂^0​⊳¯​f​(𝐱,t)=∂f​(𝐱,t)∂t.\hat{\partial}_{0}\,\bar{\triangleright}\,f(\mathbf{x},t)=\frac{\partial f(\mathbf{x},t)}{\partial t}.(272)

We note that the operator representations in Eq. (271) are tied
to the normal ordering defined in Eq. (252) of the previous chapter.

We can also shiftqq-deformed partial derivatives from therightside of a normal-ordered monomial to the left side using the Leibniz
rules. This procedure leads to the so-calledright-representations of partial derivatives, which are denoted byf​⊲¯​∂if\,\bar{\triangleleft}\,\partial_{i}orf⊲∂^if\triangleleft\hat{\partial}_{i}[55].

The operator representations in Eqs. (262) and
(271) split into two parts∂A⊳F=(∂A,cla+∂A,cor)⊳F.\partial_{A}\triangleright F=\left(\partial_{A,\operatorname*{cla}}+\partial_{A,\operatorname*{cor}}\right)\triangleright F.(273)

In the limitq→1q\rightarrow 1,∂A,cla\partial_{A,\operatorname*{cla}}reduces to
the usual partial derivative, while∂A,cor\partial_{A,\operatorname*{cor}}vanishes. A solution to the difference equation∂A⊳F=f\partial^{A}\triangleright F=fcan be written as[56]:F\displaystyle F=(∂A)−1⊳f=(∂A,cla+∂A,cor)−1⊳f\displaystyle=(\partial_{A})^{-1}\triangleright f=\left(\partial_{A,\operatorname*{cla}}+\partial_{A,\operatorname*{cor}}\right)^{-1}\triangleright f=∑k=0∞[−(∂A,cla)−1⁡∂A,cor]k​(∂A,cla)−1⊳f.\displaystyle=\sum_{k\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{\infty}\left[-(\partial_{A,\operatorname*{cla}})^{-1}\partial_{A,\operatorname*{cor}}\right]^{k}(\partial_{A,\operatorname*{cla}})^{-1}\triangleright f.(274)

Applying this formula to the operator representations in
Eq. (262) yields(∂+)−1⊳f​(𝐱,t)\displaystyle(\partial_{+})^{-1}\triangleright f(\mathbf{x},t)=Dq4,x+−1​f​(𝐱,t),\displaystyle=D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{+}}^{-1}f(\mathbf{x},t),(∂3)−1⊳f​(𝐱,t)\displaystyle(\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3})^{-1}\triangleright f(\mathbf{x},t)=Dq2,x3−1​f​(q−2​x+,x3,x−,t),\displaystyle=D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{-1}f(q^{-2}x^{+},x^{3},x^{-},t),(275)

and(∂−)−1⊳f​(𝐱,t)=\displaystyle(\partial_{-})^{-1}\triangleright f(\mathbf{x},t)==∑k=0∞q2​k​(k+1)​(−λ​x+​Dq4,x−−1​Dq2,x32)k​Dq4,x−−1​f​(x+,q−2​(k+1)​x3,x−,t).\displaystyle=\sum_{k\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{\infty}q^{2k\left(k\hskip 0.72229pt+1\right)}\left(-\lambda\,x^{+}D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{-}}^{-1}D_{q^{2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{2}\right)^{k}D_{q^{4},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{-}}^{-1}f(x^{+},q^{-2\left(k\hskip 0.72229pt+1\right)}x^{3},x^{-},t).(276)

Here,Dq,x−1D_{q,\hskip 0.72229ptx}^{-1}denotes a Jackson integral with respect to
the variablexx. The explicit form of this Jackson integral depends on its
integration limits and the value of the deformation parameterqq[cf.
Eqs.(28)-(32) in Chap.2.2]. The
time coordinate integral remains unaffected byqq-deformation [cf.
Eq. (263)]:(∂0)−1⊳f​(𝐱,t)=∫d​t​f​(𝐱,t).(\partial_{0})^{-1}\triangleright f(\mathbf{x},t)\hskip 0.72229pt=\int\text{d}t\,f(\mathbf{x},t).(277)

The considerations above extend directly to the conjugate set of partial
derivatives∂^i\hat{\partial}_{i}with minor adjustments. Recall that the
representations of∂^i\hat{\partial}_{i}can be derived from those of∂i\partial_{i}by replacingqqwithq−1q^{-1}and exchanging the indices++and−-. Applying these substitutions to Eqs. (275) and
(276) yields the corresponding results for the conjugate partial
derivatives∂^i\hat{\partial}_{i}.

By successively applying the integral operators defined in
Eqs. (275) and (276), one can define anintegration over the entireqq-deformed Euclidean space[56,57]:∫−∞+∞dq3​x​f​(x+,x3,x−)=(∂−)−1|−∞+∞​(∂3)−1|−∞+∞​(∂+)−1|−∞+∞⊳f.\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\text{d}_{q}^{3}x\,f(x^{+},x^{3},x^{-})=(\partial_{-})^{-1}\big|_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,(\partial_{\hskip 0.72229pt3})^{-1}\big|_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,(\partial_{+})^{-1}\big|_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\triangleright f.(278)

On the right-hand side of the equation above, the integral operators(∂A)−1(\partial_{A})^{-1}reduce to improper Jackson integrals[56,37]:∫dq3​x​f=∫−∞+∞dq3​x​f​(𝐱)=∫−∞+∞dq2​x−​∫−∞+∞dq​x3​∫−∞+∞dq2​x+​f​(𝐱).\int\text{d}_{q}^{3}x\,f=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\text{d}_{q}^{3}x\,f(\mathbf{x})=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\text{d}_{q^{2}}x^{-}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\text{d}_{q}x^{3}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\text{d}_{q^{2}}x^{+}\,f(\mathbf{x}).(279)

These improper Jackson integrals are defined on a smallerqq-lattice so
that the full space integral has trivial braiding[34].
Moreover, theqq-integral over all space satisfies aqq-analogue of Stokes’ theorem[57,37]:∫−∞+∞dq3​x​∂A⊳f\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\text{d}_{q}^{3}x\,\partial^{A}\triangleright f=∫−∞+∞dq3​x​f​⊲¯​∂A=0,\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\text{d}_{q}^{3}x\,f\,\bar{\triangleleft}\,\partial^{A}=0,∫−∞+∞dq3​x​∂^A​⊳¯​f\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\text{d}_{q}^{3}x\,\hat{\partial}^{A}\,\bar{\triangleright}\,f=∫−∞+∞dq3​x​f⊲∂^A=0.\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\text{d}_{q}^{3}x\,f\triangleleft\hat{\partial}^{A}=0.(280)

## Appendix CExponentials and Translations onqq-Deformed Euclidean
Space

Anexponential on aqq-deformed quantum spaceis an
eigenfunction of allqq-deformed partial derivatives[30,58,59]. In particular,qq-exponentials may be viewed as eigenfunctions with respect to either
the left or right action of these derivatives:i−1​∂A⊳expq⁡(𝐱|i​𝐩)\displaystyle\text{i}^{-1}\partial^{A}\triangleright\exp_{q}(\mathbf{x}|\text{i}\mathbf{p})=expq⁡(𝐱|i​𝐩)⊛pA,\displaystyle=\exp_{q}(\mathbf{x}|\text{i}\mathbf{p})\circledast p^{A},expq⁡(i−1​𝐩|𝐱)​⊲¯​∂Ai−1\displaystyle\exp_{q}(\text{i}^{-1}\mathbf{p}|\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{x})\,\bar{\triangleleft}\,\partial^{A}\text{i}^{-1}=pA⊛expq⁡(i−1​𝐩|𝐱).\displaystyle=p^{A}\circledast\exp_{q}(\text{i}^{-1}\mathbf{p}|\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{x}).(281)

These eigenvalue equations are illustrated in Fig.1. Theqq-exponentials are uniquely determined by their eigenvalue equations
together with the normalization conditionsexpq⁡(𝐱|i​𝐩)|x=0\displaystyle\exp_{q}(\mathbf{x}|\text{i}\mathbf{p})|_{x\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}=expq⁡(𝐱|i​𝐩)|p=0=1,\displaystyle=\exp_{q}(\mathbf{x}|\text{i}\mathbf{p})|_{p\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}=1,expq⁡(i−1​𝐩|𝐱)|x=0\displaystyle\exp_{q}(\text{i}^{-1}\mathbf{p}|\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{x})|_{x\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}=expq⁡(i−1​𝐩|𝐱)|p=0=1.\displaystyle=\exp_{q}(\text{i}^{-1}\mathbf{p}|\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{x})|_{p\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}=1.(282)Figure 1:Eigenvalue equations ofqq-exponentials.

From the operator representation in Eq. (262) in the previous
chapter, we derived explicit series expansions forqq-exponentials onqq-deformed Euclidean space[59]:expq⁡(i−1​𝐩|𝐱)=\displaystyle\exp_{q}(\text{i}^{-1}\mathbf{p}|\mathbf{x})==∑n¯=0∞(i−1​p+)n+​(i−1​p3)n3​(i−1​p−)n−​(x−)n−​(x3)n3​(x+)n+[[n+]]q4!​[[n3]]q2!​[[n−]]q4!,\displaystyle=\sum_{\underline{n}\,=\,0}^{\infty}\frac{(\text{i}^{-1}p^{+})^{n_{+}}(\text{i}^{-1}p^{3})^{n_{3}}(\text{i}^{-1}p^{-})^{n_{-}}(x_{-})^{n_{-}}(x_{3})^{n_{3}}(x_{+})^{n_{+}}}{[[\hskip 0.72229ptn_{+}]]_{q^{4}}!\,[[\hskip 0.72229ptn_{3}]]_{q^{2}}!\,[[\hskip 0.72229ptn_{-}]]_{q^{4}}!},(283)

andexpq⁡(𝐱|i​𝐩)=\displaystyle\exp_{q}(\mathbf{x}|\text{i}\mathbf{p})==∑n¯=0∞(x+)n+​(x3)n3​(x−)n−​(i​p−)n−​(i​p3)n3​(i​p+)n+[[n+]]q4!​[[n3]]q2!​[[n−]]q4!.\displaystyle=\sum_{\underline{n}\,=\,0}^{\infty}\frac{(x^{+})^{n_{+}}(x^{3})^{n_{3}}(x^{-})^{n_{-}}(\text{i}p_{-})^{n_{-}}(\text{i}p_{3})^{n_{3}}(\text{i}p_{+})^{n_{+}}}{[[\hskip 0.72229ptn_{+}]]_{q^{4}}!\,[[\hskip 0.72229ptn_{3}]]_{q^{2}}!\,[[\hskip 0.72229ptn_{-}]]_{q^{4}}!}.(284)

By replacingqqwithq−1q^{-1}in Eqs. (283) and (284), one obtains two furtherqq-exponentials, denoted byexp¯q(x|\overline{\exp}_{q}(x|i𝐩)\mathbf{p})andexp¯q(\overline{\exp}_{q}(i𝐩−1|x){}^{-1}\mathbf{p}|x). Their
eigenvalue equations and normalization conditions follow directly from
Eqs. (281) and (282) upon applying the substitutionsexpq→exp¯q,⊳→⊳¯,⊲¯→⊲,∂A→∂^A.\exp_{q}\rightarrow\hskip 0.72229pt\overline{\exp}_{q},\qquad\triangleright\,\rightarrow\,\bar{\triangleright},\qquad\bar{\triangleleft}\,\rightarrow\,\triangleleft,\qquad\partial^{A}\rightarrow\hat{\partial}^{A}.(285)

We can employqq-exponentials to calculateqq-translations[31]. Substituting momentum variables by derivatives
in theqq-exponentials yields[60,30,57]:expq⁡(𝐱|∂y)⊳g​(𝐲)\displaystyle\exp_{q}(\mathbf{x}|\partial_{y})\triangleright g(\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{y})=g​(𝐱​⊕¯​𝐲),\displaystyle=g(\mathbf{x}\,\bar{\oplus}\,\mathbf{y}),exp¯q​(𝐱|∂^y)​⊳¯​g​(𝐲)\displaystyle\overline{\exp}_{q}(\mathbf{x}|\hat{\partial}_{y})\,\bar{\triangleright}\,g(\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{y})=g​(𝐱⊕𝐲),\displaystyle=g(\mathbf{x}\oplus\mathbf{y}),(286)

andg​(𝐲)​⊲¯​expq⁡(−∂y|𝐱)\displaystyle g(\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{y})\,\bar{\triangleleft}\,\exp_{q}(-\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{y}|\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{x})=g​(𝐲​⊕¯​𝐱),\displaystyle=g(\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{y}\,\bar{\oplus}\,\mathbf{x}),g​(𝐲)⊲exp¯q​(−∂^y|𝐱)\displaystyle g(\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{y})\triangleleft\hskip 0.72229pt\overline{\exp}_{q}(-\hskip 0.72229pt\hat{\partial}_{y}|\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{x})=g​(𝐲⊕𝐱).\displaystyle=g(\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{y}\oplus\mathbf{x}).(287)

For three-dimensionalqq-deformed Euclidean space, one obtains, for
example, the following formula for calculatingqq-translations[52]:f​(𝐱⊕𝐲)=\displaystyle f(\mathbf{x}\oplus\mathbf{y})==∑i+=0∞∑i3=0∞∑i−=0∞∑k=0i3(−q−1​λ​λ+)k[[2​k]]q−2!!​(x−)i−​(x3)i3−k​(x+)i++k​(y−)k[[i−]]q−4!​[[i3−k]]q−2!​[[i+]]q−4!\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{+}=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{\infty}\sum_{i_{3}=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{\infty}\sum_{i_{-}=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{\infty}\sum_{k\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{i_{3}}\frac{(-q^{-1}\lambda\lambda_{+})^{k}}{[[2k]]_{q^{-2}}!!}\frac{(x^{-})^{i_{-}}(x^{3})^{i_{3}-\hskip 0.72229ptk}(x^{+})^{i_{+}+\hskip 0.72229ptk}\,(\hskip 0.72229pty^{-})^{k}}{[[i_{-}]]_{q^{-4}}!\,[[i_{3}-k]]_{q^{-2}}!\,[[i_{+}]]_{q^{-4}}!}×(Dq−4,y−i−​Dq−2,y3i3+k​Dq−4,y+i+​f)​(q2​(k−i3)​y−,q−2​i+​y3).\displaystyle\times\big(D_{q^{-4},\hskip 0.72229pty^{-}}^{i_{-}}D_{q^{-2},\hskip 0.72229pty^{3}}^{i_{3}+\hskip 0.72229ptk}\hskip 0.72229ptD_{q^{-4},\hskip 0.72229pty^{+}}^{i_{+}}f\big)(q^{2(k\hskip 0.72229pt-\hskip 0.72229pti_{3})}y^{-},q^{-2i_{+}}y^{3}).(288)

In analogy with the undeformed case,qq-exponentials satisfyaddition theorems[30,58,57]expq⁡(𝐱​⊕¯​𝐲|i​𝐩)\displaystyle\exp_{q}(\mathbf{x}\,\bar{\oplus}\,\mathbf{y}|\text{i}\mathbf{p})=expq⁡(𝐱|expq⁡(𝐲∣i​𝐩)⊛i​𝐩),\displaystyle=\exp_{q}(\mathbf{x}|\exp_{q}(\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{y}|\text{i}\mathbf{p})\circledast\text{i}\mathbf{p}),expq⁡(i​𝐱|𝐩​⊕¯​𝐩′)\displaystyle\exp_{q}(\text{i}\mathbf{x}|\mathbf{p}\,\bar{\oplus}\,\mathbf{p}^{\prime})=expq⁡(𝐱⊛expq⁡(𝐱|i​𝐩)|i​𝐩′),\displaystyle=\exp_{q}(\mathbf{x}\circledast\exp_{q}(\mathbf{x}|\hskip 0.72229pt\text{i}\mathbf{p})|\hskip 0.72229pt\text{i}\mathbf{p}^{\prime}),(289)

andexp¯q​(𝐱⊕𝐲|i​𝐩)\displaystyle\overline{\exp}_{q}(\mathbf{x}\oplus\mathbf{y}|\text{i}\mathbf{p})=exp¯q(𝐱|exp¯q(𝐲|i𝐩)⊛i𝐩),\displaystyle=\overline{\exp}_{q}(\mathbf{x}|\overline{\exp}_{q}(\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{y}|\text{i}\mathbf{p})\circledast\text{i}\mathbf{p}),exp¯q​(i​𝐱|𝐩⊕𝐩′)\displaystyle\overline{\exp}_{q}(\text{i}\mathbf{x}|\mathbf{p}\oplus\mathbf{p}^{\prime})=exp¯q​(𝐱⊛exp¯q​(𝐱|i​𝐩)|i​𝐩′).\displaystyle=\overline{\exp}_{q}(\mathbf{x}\circledast\overline{\exp}_{q}(\mathbf{x}|\text{i}\mathbf{p})|\hskip 0.72229pt\text{i}\mathbf{p}^{\prime}).(290)

Additional addition theorems can be derived by interchanging position and
momentum variables. Graphical representations of the two addition theorems in
Eq. (289) are shown in Fig.2.Figure 2:Addition theorems forqq-exponentials.

Theqq-deformed quantum spaces provide natural examples ofbraided
Hopf algebras[61]. Within this framework, the two distinct
forms ofqq-translations arise from representations of two braided
coproducts, denotedΔ¯\underline{\Delta}andΔ¯¯\underline{\bar{\Delta}},
acting on the associated commutative coordinate algebras[57]:f​(𝐱⊕𝐲)\displaystyle f(\mathbf{x}\oplus\mathbf{y})=((𝒲−1⊗𝒲−1)∘Δ¯)​(𝒲​(f)),\displaystyle=((\mathcal{W}^{\hskip 0.72229pt-1}\otimes\mathcal{W}^{\hskip 0.72229pt-1})\circ\underline{\Delta})(\mathcal{W}(f)),f​(𝐱​⊕¯​𝐲)\displaystyle f(\mathbf{x}\,\bar{\oplus}\,\mathbf{y})=((𝒲−1⊗𝒲−1)∘Δ¯¯)​(𝒲​(f)).\displaystyle=((\mathcal{W}^{\hskip 0.72229pt-1}\otimes\mathcal{W}^{-1})\circ\underline{\bar{\Delta}})(\mathcal{W}(f)).(291)

Braided Hopf algebras admit braided antipodes, denotedS¯\underline{S}andS¯¯\underline{\bar{S}}. These antipodes induce representations on the
commutative algebras:f​(⊖𝐱)\displaystyle f(\ominus\,\mathbf{x})=(𝒲−1∘S¯)​(𝒲​(f)),\displaystyle=(\mathcal{W}^{\hskip 0.72229pt-1}\circ\underline{S}\hskip 0.72229pt)(\mathcal{W}(f)),f​(⊖¯​𝐱)\displaystyle f(\bar{\ominus}\,\mathbf{x})=(𝒲−1∘S¯¯)​(𝒲​(f)).\displaystyle=(\mathcal{W}^{\hskip 0.72229pt-1}\circ\underline{\bar{S}}\hskip 0.72229pt)(\mathcal{W}(f)).(292)

We refer to the operations in Eq. (292) asqq-inversions. For theqq-deformed Euclidean space, one finds
the following operator representation[52]:U^−1​f​(⊖𝐱)=\displaystyle\hat{U}^{-1}f(\ominus\,\mathbf{x})==∑i=0∞(−q​λ​λ+)i​(x+​x−)i[[2​i]]q−2!!​q−2​n^+​(n^++n^3)−2​n^−​(n^−+n^3)−n^3​n^3\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}(-\hskip 0.72229ptq\lambda\lambda_{+})^{i}\,\frac{(x^{+}x^{-})^{i}}{[[2i]]_{q^{-2}}!!}\,q^{-2\hat{n}_{+}(\hat{n}_{+}+\hskip 0.72229pt\hat{n}_{3})-2\hat{n}_{-}(\hat{n}_{-}+\hskip 0.72229pt\hat{n}_{3})-\hat{n}_{3}\hat{n}_{3}}×Dq−2,x32​i​f​(−q2−4​i​x−,−q1−2​i​x3,−q2−4​i​x+).\displaystyle\times D_{q^{-2},\hskip 0.72229ptx^{3}}^{\hskip 0.72229pt2i}\,f(-\hskip 0.72229ptq^{2-4i}x^{-},-\hskip 0.72229ptq^{1-2i}x^{3},-\hskip 0.72229ptq^{2-4i}x^{+}).(293)

The braided coproducts and braided antipodes satisfy theHopf algebra
axioms(see Ref.[61]):m∘(S¯⊗id)∘Δ¯\displaystyle m\circ(\underline{S}\otimes\operatorname*{id})\circ\underline{\Delta}=m∘(id⊗S¯)∘Δ¯=ε¯,\displaystyle=m\circ(\operatorname*{id}\otimes\,\underline{S}\hskip 0.72229pt)\circ\underline{\Delta}=\underline{\varepsilon},m∘(S¯¯⊗id)∘Δ¯¯\displaystyle m\circ(\underline{\bar{S}}\otimes\operatorname*{id})\circ\underline{\bar{\Delta}}=m∘(id⊗S¯¯)∘Δ¯¯=ε¯¯,\displaystyle=m\circ(\operatorname*{id}\otimes\,\underline{\bar{S}}\hskip 0.72229pt)\circ\underline{\bar{\Delta}}=\underline{\bar{\varepsilon}},(294)

and(id⊗ε¯)∘Δ¯\displaystyle(\operatorname*{id}\otimes\,\underline{\varepsilon})\circ\underline{\Delta}=id=(ε¯⊗id)∘Δ¯,\displaystyle=\operatorname*{id}=(\underline{\varepsilon}\otimes\operatorname*{id})\circ\underline{\Delta},(id⊗ε¯¯)∘Δ¯¯\displaystyle(\operatorname*{id}\otimes\,\underline{\bar{\varepsilon}})\circ\underline{\bar{\Delta}}=id=(ε¯¯⊗id)∘Δ¯¯.\displaystyle=\operatorname*{id}=(\underline{\bar{\varepsilon}}\otimes\operatorname*{id})\circ\underline{\bar{\Delta}}.(295)

Here,mmdenotes multiplication in the braided Hopf algebra, whileε¯\underline{\varepsilon}orε¯¯\underline{\bar{\varepsilon}}are the
corresponding counits. These counits act as linear maps annihilating the
coordinate generators:ε​(Xi)=ε¯¯​(Xi)=0.\varepsilon(X^{i})=\underline{\bar{\varepsilon}}(X^{i})=0.(296)

Accordingly, on a commutative coordinate algebra they are represented by
evaluation at the origin:ε¯​(𝒲​(f))=ε¯¯​(𝒲​(f))=f​(𝐱)|x=0=f​(0).\underline{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{W}(f))=\underline{\bar{\varepsilon}}(\mathcal{W}(f))=\left.f(\mathbf{x})\right|_{x\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}=f(0).(297)

Within this setting, the Hopf algebra axioms (294) and
(295) naturally translate into the following rules forqq-translations andqq-inversions[57]:f​((⊖𝐱)⊕𝐱)\displaystyle f((\ominus\,\mathbf{x})\oplus\mathbf{x})=f​(𝐱⊕(⊖𝐱))=f​(0),\displaystyle=f(\mathbf{x}\oplus(\ominus\,\mathbf{x}))=f(0),f​((⊖¯​𝐱)​⊕¯​𝐱)\displaystyle f((\bar{\ominus}\,\mathbf{x})\,\bar{\oplus}\,\mathbf{x})=f​(𝐱​⊕¯​(⊖¯​𝐱))=f​(0),\displaystyle=f(\mathbf{x}\,\bar{\oplus}\,(\bar{\ominus}\,\mathbf{x}))=f(0),(298)

andf​(𝐱⊕𝐲)|y=0\displaystyle f(\mathbf{x}\oplus\mathbf{y})|_{y\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}=f​(𝐱)=f​(𝐲⊕𝐱)|y=0,\displaystyle=f(\mathbf{x})=f(\mathbf{y}\oplus\mathbf{x})|_{y\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0},f​(𝐱​⊕¯​𝐲)|y=0\displaystyle f(\mathbf{x}\,\bar{\oplus}\,\mathbf{y})|_{y\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}=f​(𝐱)=f​(𝐲​⊕¯​𝐱)|y=0.\displaystyle=f(\mathbf{x})=f(\mathbf{y}\,\bar{\oplus}\,\mathbf{x})|_{y\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}.(299)

Based onqq-inversions, we defineinverseqq-exponentialsasexpq⁡(⊖¯​𝐱|i​𝐩)=expq⁡(i​𝐱|​⊖¯​𝐩).\exp_{q}(\bar{\ominus}\,\mathbf{x}|\text{i}\mathbf{p})=\exp_{q}(\text{i}\mathbf{x}|\text{{}}\bar{\ominus}\,\mathbf{p}).(300)

By employing the addition theorems, the identities in Eq. (298), and
the normalization conditions of ourqq-exponentials, one obtains:expq⁡(i​𝐱⊛expq⁡(⊖¯​𝐱|i​𝐩)⊛𝐩)=expq⁡(𝐱​⊕¯​(⊖¯​𝐱)|i​𝐩)=expq⁡(𝐱|i​𝐩)|x=0=1.\exp_{q}(\text{i}\mathbf{x}\circledast\exp_{q}(\bar{\ominus}\,\mathbf{x}|\hskip 0.72229pt\text{i}\mathbf{p})\circledast\mathbf{p})=\exp_{q}(\mathbf{x}\,\bar{\oplus}\,(\bar{\ominus}\,\mathbf{x})|\hskip 0.72229pt\text{i}\mathbf{p})=\exp_{q}(\mathbf{x}|\text{i}\mathbf{p})|_{x=0}=1.(301)

Graphical representations of these identities are shown in Fig.3.111111Further details of these graphical calculations can be found in
Ref.[62].Figure 3:Invertibility ofqq-exponentials.

It is often convenient to exchange the tensor factors in the inverseqq-exponentials of Eq. (300) using the inverse of the
universal R-matrix (see the graphical representation in Fig.4):expq∗⁡(i​𝐩|𝐱)\displaystyle\exp_{q}^{\ast}(\text{i}\mathbf{p}|\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{x})=τ∘[(ℛ[2]−1⊗ℛ[1]−1)⊳expq⁡(i​𝐱|⊖𝐩)],\displaystyle=\tau\circ[(\mathcal{R}_{[2]}^{-1}\otimes\mathcal{R}_{[1]}^{-1})\triangleright\exp_{q}(\text{i}\mathbf{x}|\hskip-2.168pt\ominus\hskip-0.72229pt\mathbf{p})],expq∗⁡(𝐱|i​𝐩)\displaystyle\exp_{q}^{\ast}(\mathbf{x}|\text{i}\mathbf{p})=τ∘[(ℛ[2]−1⊗ℛ[1]−1)⊳expq⁡(⊖𝐩|i​𝐱)].\displaystyle=\tau\circ[(\mathcal{R}_{[2]}^{-1}\otimes\mathcal{R}_{[1]}^{-1})\triangleright\exp_{q}(\ominus\hskip 1.4457pt\mathbf{p}|\hskip 0.72229pt\text{i}\mathbf{x})].(302)

Here,τ\taudenotes the ordinary twist operator. Thesetwistedqq-exponentialssatisfy the following eigenvalue equations (cf.
Fig.4):expq∗⁡(i​𝐩|𝐱)⊲∂A\displaystyle\exp_{q}^{\ast}(\text{i}\mathbf{p}|\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{x})\triangleleft\partial^{A}=i​pA⊛expq∗⁡(i​𝐩|𝐱),\displaystyle=\text{i}p^{A}\circledast\exp_{q}^{\ast}(\text{i}\mathbf{p}|\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{x}),∂A⊳¯​expq∗⁡(𝐱|i−1​𝐩)\displaystyle\partial^{A}\,\bar{\triangleright}\,\exp_{q}^{\ast}(\mathbf{x}|\text{i}^{-1}\mathbf{p})=expq∗⁡(𝐱|i−1​𝐩)⊛i​pA.\displaystyle=\exp_{q}^{\ast}(\mathbf{x}|\text{i}^{-1}\mathbf{p})\circledast\text{i}p^{A}.(303)Figure 4:Eigenvalue equation of twistedqq-exponential.

## Appendix Dqq-Deformed Position and Momentum
Eigenfunctions

Usingqq-exponentials, we defineqq-deformed momentum eigenfunctions[43]:u𝐩​(𝐱)=vol−1/2expq⁡(𝐱|i​𝐩),u𝐩​(𝐱)=vol−1/2expq⁡(i−1​𝐩|𝐱).u_{\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})=\operatorname*{vol}\nolimits^{-1/2}\exp_{q}(\mathbf{x}|\text{i}\mathbf{p}),\qquad u^{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})=\operatorname*{vol}\nolimits^{-1/2}\exp_{q}(\text{i}^{-1}\mathbf{p}|\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{x}).(304)

The volume elementvol\operatorname*{vol}is specified in Eq. (115)
of Chap.3.1. Theseqq-deformed momentum eigenfunctions
satisfy [cf. Eq. (281) in App.C]i−1​∂A⊳u𝐩​(𝐱)\displaystyle\text{i}^{-1}\partial^{A}\triangleright u_{\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})=u𝐩​(𝐱)⊛pA,\displaystyle=u_{\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})\circledast p^{A},u𝐩​(𝐱)​⊲¯​∂Ai−1\displaystyle u^{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})\,\bar{\triangleleft}\,\partial^{A}\hskip 0.72229pt\text{i}^{-1}=pA⊛u𝐩​(𝐱).\displaystyle=p^{A}\circledast u^{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x}).(305)

We also introduce the dual momentum eigenfunctions [cf. Eq. (302)
in App.C]:(u∗)𝐩​(𝐱)=vol−1/2expq∗⁡(i​𝐩|𝐱),(u∗)𝐩​(𝐱)=vol−1/2expq∗⁡(𝐱|i−1​𝐩).(u^{\ast})_{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})=\operatorname*{vol}\nolimits^{-1/2}\exp_{q}^{\ast}(\text{i}\mathbf{p}|\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{x}),\qquad(u^{\ast})^{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})=\operatorname*{vol}\nolimits^{-1/2}\exp_{q}^{\ast}(\mathbf{x}|\text{i}^{-1}\mathbf{p}).(306)

Their eigenvalue equations read [cf. Eq. (303) in
App.C]:(u∗)𝐩​(𝐱)⊲∂Ai−1\displaystyle(u^{\ast})_{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})\triangleleft\partial^{A}\hskip 0.72229pt\text{i}^{-1}\hskip-0.72229pt=pA⊛(u∗)𝐩​(𝐱),\displaystyle=p^{A}\circledast(u^{\ast})_{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x}),i−1​∂A⊳¯​(u∗)𝐩​(𝐱)\displaystyle\text{i}^{-1}\partial^{A}\,\bar{\triangleright}\,(u^{\ast})^{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})=(u∗)𝐩​(𝐱)⊛pA.\displaystyle=(u^{\ast})^{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})\circledast p^{A}.(307)

Theqq-deformed momentum eigenfunctions satisfy thecompleteness
relations[34,43]∫dq3​p​u𝐩​(𝐱)⊛(u∗)𝐩​(𝐲)\displaystyle\int\text{d}_{q}^{3}p\,u_{\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})\circledast(u^{\ast})_{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{y})=vol−1δq3​(𝐱⊕(⊖κ−1​𝐲)),\displaystyle=\operatorname*{vol}\nolimits^{-1}\hskip-0.72229pt\delta_{q}^{3}(\mathbf{x}\oplus(\ominus\hskip 0.72229pt\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{y})),∫dq3​p​(u∗)𝐩​(𝐲)⊛u𝐩​(𝐱)\displaystyle\int\text{d}_{q}^{3}p\,(u^{\ast})^{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{y})\circledast u^{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})=vol−1δq3​((⊖κ−1​𝐲)⊕𝐱),\displaystyle=\operatorname*{vol}\nolimits^{-1}\hskip-0.72229pt\delta_{q}^{3}((\ominus\hskip 0.72229pt\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{y})\oplus\mathbf{x}),(308)

whereδq3​(𝐱)\delta_{q}^{\hskip 0.72229pt3}(\mathbf{x})denotes theqq-deformed
version of the three-dimensional delta function [cf.
Eq. (111) in Chap.3.1]. The correspondingorthogonality relationsare:∫dq3​x​(u∗)𝐩​(𝐱)⊛u𝐩′​(𝐱)\displaystyle\int\text{d}_{q}^{3}x\,(u^{\ast})_{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})\circledast u_{\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{p}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})=vol−1δq3​((⊖κ−1​𝐩)⊕𝐩′)\displaystyle=\operatorname*{vol}\nolimits^{-1}\hskip-0.72229pt\delta_{q}^{\hskip 0.72229pt3}((\ominus\hskip 0.72229pt\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{p})\oplus\mathbf{p}^{\prime})∫dq3​x​u𝐩​(𝐱)⊛(u∗)𝐩′​(𝐱)\displaystyle\int\text{d}_{q}^{3}x\,u^{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})\circledast(u^{\ast})^{\mathbf{p}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})=vol−1δq3​(𝐩⊕(⊖κ−1​𝐩′)).\displaystyle=\operatorname*{vol}\nolimits^{-1}\hskip-0.72229pt\delta_{q}^{\hskip 0.72229pt3}(\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{p}\oplus(\ominus\hskip 0.72229pt\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{p}^{\prime})).(309)

Theqq-deformed position eigenfunctionsare defined in terms ofqq-deformed delta functions[43]:u𝐲​(𝐱)\displaystyle u_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})=vol−1δq3​(𝐱⊕(⊖κ−1​𝐲))=vol−1δq3​((⊖κ−1​𝐱)⊕𝐲)=(u∗)𝐲​(𝐱),\displaystyle=\operatorname*{vol}\nolimits^{-1}\hskip-0.72229pt\delta_{q}^{3}(\mathbf{x}\oplus(\ominus\hskip 0.72229pt\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{y}))=\operatorname*{vol}\nolimits^{-1}\hskip-0.72229pt\delta_{q}^{3}((\ominus\hskip 0.72229pt\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{x})\oplus\mathbf{y})=(u^{\ast})^{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x}),(u∗)𝐲​(𝐱)\displaystyle(u^{\ast})_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})=vol−1δq3​(𝐲⊕(⊖κ−1​𝐱))=vol−1δq3​((⊖κ−1​𝐲)⊕𝐱)=u𝐲​(𝐱).\displaystyle=\operatorname*{vol}\nolimits^{-1}\hskip-0.72229pt\delta_{q}^{3}(\mathbf{y}\oplus(\ominus\hskip 0.72229pt\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{x}))=\operatorname*{vol}\nolimits^{-1}\hskip-0.72229pt\delta_{q}^{3}((\ominus\hskip 0.72229pt\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{y})\oplus\mathbf{x})=u^{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x}).(310)

The position operators act by multiplication operators in position space:XA⊳f​(x)=xA⊛f​(x),f​(x)⊲XA=f​(x)⊛xA.X^{A}\triangleright f(x)=x^{A}\circledast f(x),\qquad f(x)\triangleleft X^{A}=f(x)\circledast x^{A}.(311)

Hence, the position eigenfunctions satisfy:XA⊳u𝐲​(𝐱)\displaystyle X^{A}\triangleright u_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})=xA⊛u𝐲​(𝐱)=u𝐲​(𝐱)⊛yA,\displaystyle=x^{A}\circledast u_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})=u_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})\circledast y^{A},(u∗)𝐲​(𝐱)⊲XA\displaystyle(u^{\ast})_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})\triangleleft X^{A}=(u∗)𝐲​(𝐱)⊛xA=yA⊛(u∗)𝐲​(𝐱).\displaystyle=(u^{\ast})_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})\circledast x^{A}=y^{A}\circledast(u^{\ast})_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x}).(312)

Theorthogonality relationsforqq-deformed position
eigenfunctions read∫dq3​x​u𝐲​(𝐱)⊛(u∗)𝐲′​(𝐱)=∫dq3​x​(u∗)𝐲​(𝐱)⊛u𝐲′​(𝐱)\displaystyle\int\text{d}_{q}^{3}x\,u^{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})\circledast(u^{\ast})^{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})=\int\text{d}_{q}^{3}x\,(u^{\ast})_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})\circledast u_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})=vol−1δq3​(𝐲⊕(⊖κ−1​𝐲′)).\displaystyle=\operatorname*{vol}\nolimits^{-1}\hskip-0.72229pt\delta_{q}^{3}(\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{y}\oplus(\ominus\hskip 0.72229pt\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{y}^{\prime})).(313)

The correspondingcompleteness relationsare∫dq3​y​(u∗)𝐲​(𝐱′)⊛u𝐲​(𝐱)=∫dq3​y​u𝐲​(𝐱′)⊛(u∗)𝐲​(𝐱)\displaystyle\int\text{d}_{q}^{3}\hskip 0.72229pty\,(u^{\ast})^{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\circledast u^{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})=\int\text{d}_{q}^{3}\hskip 0.72229pty\,u_{\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\circledast(u^{\ast})_{\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{x})=vol−1δq3​(𝐱′⊕(⊖κ−1​𝐱)).\displaystyle=\operatorname*{vol}\nolimits^{-1}\hskip-0.72229pt\delta_{q}^{3}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\oplus(\ominus\hskip 0.72229pt\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{x})).(314)

## Appendix Eqq-Deformed Klein-Gordon Equation

In Ref.[63]we introduced aqq-deformed analogue
of the Klein-Gordon equationdescribing a spinless particle of rest massmmin three-dimensionalqq-deformed Euclidean space:c−2​∂t2⊳φ​(𝐱,t)−∇q2⊳φ​(𝐱,t)+(m​c)2​φ​(𝐱,t)=0.c^{-2}\partial_{t}^{\hskip 0.72229pt2}\triangleright\varphi(\mathbf{x},t)-\hskip-0.72229pt\nabla_{q}^{2}\triangleright\varphi(\mathbf{x},t)+(m\hskip 0.72229ptc)^{2}\hskip 0.72229pt\varphi(\mathbf{x},t)=0.(315)

Theqq-deformed Laplacian∇q2\nabla_{q}^{2}depends on the metric of
three-dimensionalqq-deformed Euclidean space [cf.
Eq. (239) in App.A]:∇q2=Δq=∂∘∂=∂A∂A=gA​B​∂B∂A.\nabla_{q}^{2}=\Delta_{q}=\mathbf{\partial}\circ\mathbf{\partial}=\partial^{A}\partial_{A}=g^{AB}\partial_{B}\hskip 0.72229pt\partial_{A}.(316)

Theqq-deformed Klein-Gordon equation in Eq. (315) admitsplane wave solutionsof the formφ𝐩​(𝐱,t)=c2​u𝐩​(𝐱)⊛exp⁡(−i​t​E𝐩)⊛E𝐩−1/2,\varphi_{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x},t)=\frac{c}{\sqrt{2}}\,u_{\hskip 0.72229pt\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{x})\circledast\exp(-\text{i\hskip 0.72229pt}tE_{\mathbf{p}})\circledast E_{\mathbf{p}}^{\hskip 0.72229pt-1/2},(317)

with the time-dependent phase factor represented byexp⁡(−i​t​E𝐩)=∑n=0∞(−i​t​E𝐩)nn!.\exp(-\hskip 0.72229pt\text{i\hskip 0.72229pt}tE_{\mathbf{p}})=\sum_{n\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{\infty}\frac{(-\hskip 0.72229pt\text{i\hskip 0.72229pt}tE_{\mathbf{p}})^{n}}{n!}.(318)

Substituting Eq. (317) into Eq. (315) yields0\displaystyle 0=c−2​∂t2⊳φ𝐩−∇q2⊳φ𝐩+(m​c)2​φ𝐩\displaystyle=c^{-2}\partial_{t}^{\hskip 0.72229pt2}\triangleright\varphi_{\mathbf{p}}-\nabla_{q}^{2}\triangleright\varphi_{\mathbf{p}}+(m\hskip 0.72229ptc)^{2}\varphi_{\mathbf{p}}=φ𝐩⊛(pB⊛pB−c−2​E𝐩⊛E𝐩+(m​c)2),\displaystyle=\varphi_{\mathbf{p}}\circledast(\hskip 0.72229ptp^{B}\hskip-0.72229pt\circledast p_{B}-c^{-2}E_{\mathbf{p}}\circledast E_{\mathbf{p}}+(m\hskip 0.72229ptc)^{2}),(319)

provided that the followingqq-deformed dispersion relationholds:c−2​E𝐩⊛E𝐩=pB⊛pB+(m​c)2.c^{-2}E_{\mathbf{p}}\circledast E_{\mathbf{p}}=p^{B}\hskip-0.72229pt\circledast p_{B}+(m\hskip 0.72229ptc)^{2}.(320)

Sincem2m^{2}and𝐩2=pB⊛pB\mathbf{p}^{2}=p^{B}\hskip-0.72229pt\circledast p_{B}commute, this relation can be solved formally forE𝐩E_{\mathbf{p}}:E𝐩\displaystyle E_{\mathbf{p}}=c​(𝐩2+(m​c)2)1/2\displaystyle=c\,(\mathbf{p}^{2}+(m\hskip 0.72229ptc)^{2})^{1/2}=c​∑k=0∞(1/2k)​𝐩2​k​(m​c)1−2​k,\displaystyle=c\sum_{k\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{\infty}\binom{1/2}{k}\,\mathbf{p}^{2k}(m\hskip 0.72229ptc)^{1-\hskip 0.72229pt2k},(321)

where the powers of𝐩2\mathbf{p}^{2}must be replaced by normal-ordered
expressions[64]:𝐩2​k\displaystyle\mathbf{p}^{2k}=𝐩2⊛…⊛𝐩2⏞k−times\displaystyle=\hskip 0.72229pt\overset{k-\text{times}}{\overbrace{\mathbf{p}^{2}\circledast\ldots\circledast\mathbf{p}^{2}}}=∑l=0kq−2​l​(−q−q−1)k−l​[kl]q4​(p−)k−l​(p3)2​l​(p+)k−l.\displaystyle=\sum_{l\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{k}\hskip 0.72229ptq^{-2l}(-q-q^{-1})^{k-l}\genfrac{\left[}{]}{0.0pt}{}{k}{l}_{q^{4}}\,(\hskip 0.72229ptp_{-})^{k-l}(\hskip 0.72229ptp_{3})^{2l}(\hskip 0.72229ptp_{+})^{k-l}.(322)

## Appendix FDerivation of Auxiliary Formulas

In this appendix, we derive several auxiliary formulas needed in
Chap.4.4for evaluating the expectation value⟨H2⟩Sm\langle H^{2}\rangle_{S_{m}}[cf. Eq. (206) in Chap.4.4].
Inspection of Eqs. (213), (216), (218) and
(219) in Chap.4.4, together with
Eq. (149) from Chap.4.2, shows that
expressions of the form below occur repeatedly (m,n∈ℤm,n\in\mathbb{Z}):∑ε,ε′=±∑k,l=−∞m|(1−q)​x|​ϕq​(qn​x,y)|x=ε​α​qk,y=ε′​α​ql=\displaystyle\sum_{\varepsilon,\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}=\hskip 0.72229pt\pm}\,\sum_{k,\hskip 0.72229ptl\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m}\left.\left|(1-q)\hskip 0.72229ptx\right|\,\phi_{q}(q^{n}x,y)\right|_{{}_{x\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k},\,y\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{l}}}==∑ε,ε′=±∑k,l=−∞m|(1−q)​ε​α​qk|​ϕq​(ε​α​qk+n,ε′​α​ql)\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{\varepsilon,\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}=\hskip 0.72229pt\pm}\,\sum_{k,\hskip 0.72229ptl\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m}\left|(1-q)\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k}\right|\,\phi_{q}(\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k+n},\varepsilon^{\hskip 0.72229pt\prime}\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{l})=∑ε,ε′=±∑k,l=−∞m(q−1)​α​qk​δε,ε′​δk+n,l\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{\varepsilon,\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}=\hskip 0.72229pt\pm}\,\sum_{k,\hskip 0.72229ptl\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m}(q-1)\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k}\hskip 0.72229pt\delta_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}}\hskip 0.72229pt\delta_{k+n,l}=2​(q−1)​α​∑k,l=−∞mqk​δk+n,l.\displaystyle\qquad=2(q-1)\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\sum_{k,\hskip 0.72229ptl\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m}q^{k}\hskip 0.72229pt\delta_{k+n,l}.(323)

In this calculation, we first substituted theqq-lattice values forxxandyy, and then used the properties of the functionϕq\phi_{q}[cf.
Eqs. (77) and (78) in Chap.2.3]. To
ensure convergence of the infinite series, we assumeq>1q>1:∑k,l=−∞mqk​δk+n,l={∑k=−∞m−nqkifn>0,∑k=−∞mqkifn≤0=qm−Θ​(n)⋅n+1q−1,\sum_{k,\hskip 0.72229ptl\hskip 0.72229pt=-\infty}^{m}q^{k}\hskip 0.72229pt\delta_{k+n,l}=\left\{\begin{array}[c]{c}\sum\limits_{k\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m-n}q^{k}\quad\text{if}\quad n>0,\\
\sum\limits_{k\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m}q^{k}\quad\text{if}\quad n\leq 0\end{array}\right.=\frac{q^{m-\Theta(n)\cdot n+1}}{q-1},(324)

whereΘ​(x)\Theta(x)denotes the Heaviside step function. Eq. (324) follows directly from the formula for a geometric
series:∑k=−∞m−Θ​(n)⋅nqk=qm−Θ​(n)⋅n​∑k=−∞0qk=qm−Θ​(n)⋅n​∑k=0∞q−k=qm−Θ​(n)⋅n+1q−1.\sum_{k\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m-\Theta(n)\cdot n}q^{k}=q^{m-\Theta(n)\cdot n}\sum_{k\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{0}q^{k}=q^{m-\Theta(n)\cdot n}\sum_{k\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt0}^{\infty}q^{-k}=\frac{q^{m-\Theta(n)\cdot n+1}}{q-1}.(325)

Substituting Eq. (324) into Eq. (323), we obtain∑ε,ε′=±∑k,l=−∞m|(1−q)​x|​ϕq​(qn​x,y)|x=ε​α​qky=ε′​α​ql=2​α​qm−Θ​(n)⋅n+1.\sum_{\varepsilon,\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}=\hskip 0.72229pt\pm}\,\sum_{k,\hskip 0.72229ptl\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m}\left.\left|(1-q)\hskip 0.72229ptx\right|\,\phi_{q}(q^{n}x,y)\right|_{{}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k}\\
y\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{l}\end{subarray}}}=2\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{m-\Theta(n)\cdot n+1}.(326)

In the evaluation of⟨H2⟩Sm\langle H^{2}\rangle_{S_{m}}one also encounters sums
of the type below [cf. Eq. (214) in Chap.4.4together with Eq. (149) in Chap.4.2]:∑ε,ε′=±∑k,l=−∞m|(1−q)​x|​Dq2,x​ϕq​(qn​x,y)|x=ε​α​qk,y=ε′​α​ql=\displaystyle\sum_{\varepsilon,\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}=\hskip 0.72229pt\pm}\,\sum_{k,\hskip 0.72229ptl\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m}\left.\left|(1-q)\hskip 0.72229ptx\right|\,D_{q^{2},x}\hskip 0.72229pt\phi_{q}(q^{n}x,y)\right|_{{}_{x\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k},\,y\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{l}}}==∑ε,ε′=±∑k,l=−∞m|(1−q)​x|​ϕq​(qn+2​x,y)−ϕq​(qn​x,y)(q2−1)​x|x=ε​α​qky=ε′​α​ql\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{\varepsilon,\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}=\pm}\,\sum_{k,\hskip 0.72229ptl\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m}\left.\left|(1-q)\hskip 0.72229ptx\right|\,\frac{\phi_{q}(q^{n+2}x,y)-\phi_{q}(q^{n}x,y)}{(q^{2}-1)\hskip 0.72229ptx}\right|_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k}\\
y\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{l}\end{subarray}}=∑ε,ε′=±∑k,l=−∞m|(1−q)​ε​α​qk|​ϕq​(ε​α​qk+n+2,ε′​α​ql)−ϕq​(ε​α​qk+n,ε′​α​ql)ε​α​qk​(q2−1)\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{\varepsilon,\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}=\hskip 0.72229pt\pm}\,\sum_{k,\hskip 0.72229ptl\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m}\left|(1-q)\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k}\right|\,\frac{\phi_{q}(\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k+n+2},\varepsilon^{\hskip 0.72229pt\prime}\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{l})-\phi_{q}(\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k+n},\varepsilon^{\hskip 0.72229pt\prime}\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{l})}{\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k}(q^{2}-1)}=∑ε,ε′=±∑k,l=−∞mδε,ε′​(δk+n+2,l−δk+n,l)ε​(q+1)=0.\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{\varepsilon,\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}=\hskip 0.72229pt\pm}\,\sum_{k,\hskip 0.72229ptl\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m}\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}}(\delta_{k+n+2,l}-\delta_{k+n,l})}{\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt(q+1)}=0.(327)

In the above derivation, we expanded the Jackson derivative [cf.
Eq. (4) in Chap.2.1], substituted theqq-lattice values for both coordinates, and applied the properties of the
functionϕq\phi_{q}[cf. Eqs. (77) and (78) in
Chap.2.3]. The final equality reflects the cancellation between
the contributions forε=±\varepsilon=\pm.

Finally, we consider [cf. Eq. (216) in Chap.4.4and
Eq. (149) in Chap.4.2]:∑ε,ε′=±∑k,l=−∞m|(1−q)​x|​Dq2,x2​ϕq​(q−2​x,y)|x=ε​α​qk,y=ε′​α​ql=\displaystyle\sum_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}=\hskip 0.72229pt\pm}\,\sum_{k,\hskip 0.72229ptl\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m}\left.\left|(1-q)\hskip 0.72229ptx\right|\,D_{q^{2},x}^{2}\hskip 0.72229pt\phi_{q}(q^{-2}x,y)\right|_{{}_{x\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k},\,y\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{l}}}==∑ε,ε′=±∑k,l=−∞m(q−1)​α​qk[(1−q2)​(ε​α​qk)]2[ϕq(εαqk−2,ε′αql)\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{\varepsilon,\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}=\hskip 0.72229pt\pm}\,\sum_{k,\hskip 0.72229ptl\hskip 0.72229pt=-\infty}^{m}\frac{(q-1)\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k}}{[(1-q^{2})(\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k})]^{2}}\,\Big[\phi_{q}(\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k-2},\varepsilon^{\hskip 0.72229pt\prime}\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{l})−(1+q−2)ϕq(εαqk,ε′αql)+q−2ϕq(εαqk+2,ε′αql)]\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(1+q^{-2})\hskip 0.72229pt\phi_{q}(\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k},\varepsilon^{\hskip 0.72229pt\prime}\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{l})+q^{-2}\phi_{q}(\varepsilon\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{k\hskip 0.72229pt+2},\varepsilon^{\hskip 0.72229pt\prime}\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{l})\Big]=1(q2−1)​(q+1)​α∑ε,ε′=±δε,ε′[∑k,l=−∞mq−k(δk−2,l−δk,l)\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{(q^{2}-1)(q+1)\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha}\sum_{\varepsilon,\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}=\hskip 0.72229pt\pm}\delta_{\varepsilon,\hskip 0.72229pt\varepsilon^{\prime}}\Big[\sum_{k,\hskip 0.72229ptl\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m}q^{-k}\,(\delta_{k-2,l}-\delta_{k,l})+q−2∑k,l=−∞mq−k(δk+2,l−δk,l)]\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+q^{-2}\sum_{k,\hskip 0.72229ptl\hskip 0.72229pt=\hskip 0.72229pt-\infty}^{m}q^{-k}\,(\delta_{k\hskip 0.72229pt+2,l}-\delta_{k,l})\Big]=2(q2−1)​(q+1)​α​(−q−2)​(q−m+q−(m−1))=−2​q−2(q2−1)​α​qm.\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{2}{(q^{2}-1)(q+1)\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha}(-q^{-2})(q^{-m}+q^{-(m-1)})=-\frac{2\hskip 0.72229ptq^{-2}}{(q^{2}-1)\hskip 0.72229pt\alpha\hskip 0.72229ptq^{m}}.(328)

In this derivation, we expanded the second Jackson derivative [cf.
Eq. (4) in Chap.2.1], substituted theqq-lattice values for the coordinates, and applied the properties of the
functionϕq\phi_{q}[cf. Eqs. (77) and (78) in
Chap.2.3]. Here, the sums overε\varepsilonandε′\varepsilon^{\hskip 0.72229pt\prime}yield a factor of two, while the double sums overkkandllreduce to telescoping series: the first vanishes, and the second
collapses to the two terms−q−m-q^{-m}and−q−(m−1)-q^{-(m-1)}.
